{'role': 'user', 'content': [{'type': 'text', 'text': '<file name="D:\\dev\\ab-tasks\\_briefs\\anyio\\brief.md">\n# Task: fix a reported bug in `anyio`\r\n\r\n## Bug report (verbatim from the project\'s issue tracker)\r\n**CapacityLimiter can over-grant tokens (`borrowed_tokens > total_tokens`, `available_tokens` goes negative)**\r\n\r\n### Things to check first\r\n\r\n- [x] I have searched the existing issues and didn\'t find my bug already reported there\r\n\r\n- [x] I have checked that my bug is still present in the latest release\r\n\r\n\r\n### AnyIO version\r\n\r\n4.13.0\r\n\r\n### Python version\r\n\r\n3.12\r\n\r\n### What happened?\r\n\r\nOn the asyncio backend, a non-blocking acquire can succeed *while the just-freed token is already reserved for a woken waiter*, leaving the limiter with more borrowers than it has tokens. The state becomes:\r\n\r\n```\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2\r\ntotal_tokens     = 1\r\navailable_tokens = -1      # negative\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\n```\r\n\r\nTwo tasks then hold a single-token limiter concurrently — the exact condition the limiter exists to prevent — and `available_tokens` is negative.\r\n\r\nThis violates the documented contract of `acquire_nowait` / `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait`:\r\n\r\n> :raises ~anyio.WouldBlock: if there are no tokens available for borrowing\r\n\r\nIn the window below there is no token available (the freed one is earmarked for the woken waiter), yet the nowait acquire succeeds instead of raising `WouldBlock`.\r\n\r\n### How can we reproduce the bug?\r\n\r\nJust anyio, no test framework: `python repro.py` prints the corrupt state and raises `AssertionError`:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nimport anyio\r\nfrom anyio import CapacityLimiter, WouldBlock\r\n\r\nasync def main():\r\n    lim = CapacityLimiter(1)                       # capacity: exactly ONE token\r\n    lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("A")           # A borrows the only token\r\n\r\n    async with anyio.create_task_group() as tg:\r\n        async def b():\r\n            await lim.acquire_on_behalf_of("B")    # B waits for the token\r\n        tg.start_soon(b)\r\n\r\n        while lim.statistics().tasks_waiting != 1:  # wait until B is parked\r\n            await anyio.sleep(0)\r\n\r\n        lim.release_on_behalf_of("A")              # frees the token, notifies B\r\n        # B has been notified but has NOT resumed yet, so it is not in\r\n        # _borrowers and the wait queue is now empty. A non-blocking acquire\r\n        # in this window slips through:\r\n        try:\r\n            lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")  # expected: WouldBlock\r\n            print("X acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)")\r\n        except WouldBlock:\r\n            print("X correctly blocked")\r\n        # leaving the task group lets B resume and add itself to _borrowers\r\n\r\n    print(f"borrowed_tokens  = {lim.borrowed_tokens}  (total_tokens = {lim.total_tokens})")\r\n    print(f"available_tokens = {lim.available_tokens}")\r\n    print(f"borrowers        = {lim.statistics().borrowers}")\r\n    assert lim.borrowed_tokens <= lim.total_tokens, "borrowed_tokens > total_tokens"\r\n\r\nanyio.run(main)\r\n```\r\n\r\nOutput:\r\n\r\n```\r\nX acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2  (total_tokens = 1)\r\navailable_tokens = -1\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\nAssertionError: borrowed_tokens > total_tokens\r\n```\r\n### Cause\r\n\r\nIn `anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py`:\r\n\r\n- `release_on_behalf_of` removes the borrower then calls `_notify_next_waiter()`, which **pops** the next waiter out of `_wait_queue` and does `event.set()`.\r\n- The woken waiter, in `acquire_on_behalf_of`, only adds itself to `_borrowers` **after** `await event.wait()` returns:\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  await event.wait()\r\n  ...\r\n  self._borrowers.add(borrower)   # runs only once the waiter resumes\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n- Between the `event.set()` and the waiter resuming, `_wait_queue` is empty and the waiter is not yet in `_borrowers`. So the nowait guard\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  if self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens:\r\n      raise WouldBlock\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n  evaluates to `False` (empty queue, `0 < 1`) and admits a second borrower for the one token.\r\n\r\n### Why `Semaphore` is not affected\r\n\r\nThe sibling `Semaphore` is immune under the identical scenario, a nowait acquire issued in the same window correctly raises `WouldBlock`, because its `release()` hands the freed slot to the woken waiter rather than leaving it generally available. `CapacityLimiter` is the one primitive whose freed-but-reserved token is briefly visible as "available" to a synchronous acquirer.\r\n\r\nA fix would close that window, e.g. by reserving the token for the woken waiter at notify time (add the borrower to `_borrowers` when `_notify_next_waiter` sets its event, rather than after it resumes), mirroring the Semaphore/Lock hand-off. The nowait guard would then correctly see no free token.\r\n\r\n## Standing instructions (identical for every run)\r\nYou are working in the repository at the current working directory. Work like a careful open-source contributor:\r\n1. Reproduce the bug first (write a minimal failing test or script) before changing code.\r\n2. Find the root cause; fix the cause, not the symptom. Keep the change minimal and in the project\'s style.\r\n3. Add or update a regression test that fails before your fix and passes after.\r\n4. Run the project\'s test suite and make it green. Do not modify or delete unrelated tests.\r\n5. When done, summarize: root cause, the files you changed, how you verified it.\r\nDo not ask me for permission between steps; proceed until the task is complete, then report.\r\n\r\nTest command for this repo: `.venv/Scripts/python -m pytest tests/test_synchronization.py -q`\r\n\n</file>\n'}], 'attribution': 'user', 'timestamp': 1787268947316}

The existing `if event.is_set()` guard suggests the original author thought this race was possible (and indeed it is, on the slow path: the event\'s `_value` is set to True, then if the future is cancelled before `fut.set_result` is processed... let me look at `Event.set()`:\n\n```python\ndef set(self):\n    if not self._value:\n        self._value = True\n        for fut in self._waiters:\n            if not fut.done():\n                fut.set_result(True)\n```\n\nIf the task\'s future is cancelled (done), `set()` skips it, but `_value` is True. The task, when it resumes (from the cancellation), gets CancelledError from `await fut` (because fut.cancel() was called... wait, if the future was cancelled, `await fut` raises CancelledError). So: the waiter\'s task is cancelled → `fut.cancel()` is called (or the task\'s step cancels it) → `await fut` raises CancelledError → the except path runs → `event.is_set()` is True (because set() ran after the future was cancelled) → the token had been reserved (with the fix, the borrower was added to `_borrowers`) → must discard and re-notify.\n\nActually, more precisely: `Event.wait()`:\n\n```python\nasync def wait(self):\n    if self._value:\n        return True\n    fut = self._loop.create_future()\n    self._waiters.append(fut)\n    try:\n        await fut\n        return True\n    finally:\n        self._waiters.remove(fut)\n```\n\nSo the scenario: the waiter suspends in `await fut`. The releasing task runs `_notify_next_waiter`: pops the waiter\'s event, (with the fix) adds the borrower to `_borrowers`, `event.set()`: `_value = True`, for fut in waiters: `fut.set_result(True)` → schedules the waiter. If the waiter was already cancelled before this set() (task.cancel() was called and the future cancelled), `fut.done()` is True → set_result is skipped. When the waiter\'s task steps, it gets CancelledError. Then in the except: `event.is_set()` → True → with the fix, we must discard the borrower from `_borrowers` and notify the next waiter.\n\nSo the fix to the exception path is essential to prevent token leaks.\n\nLet me also double-check the `total_tokens` setter. Currently:\n\n```python\nwaiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)\nself._total_tokens = value\n\n# Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\nwhile self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n    event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)[1]\n    event.set()\n    waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\nWith the fix, each woken waiter must be added to `_borrowers`. We can refactor: extract a method, or just do it inline. To keep it minimal and clean, let\'s write it as:\n\n```python\nwaiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)\nself._total_tokens = value\n\n# Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\nwhile self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n    borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n    self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n    event.set()\n    waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\nHmm, but wait: is it correct to unconditionally add the borrower to `_borrowers` here? `waiters_to_notify = value - self._total_tokens` (capacity increase). After setting `_total_tokens = value`, there are `value - len(borrowers)` free slots. The number of waiters to notify is `min(len(wait_queue), value - old_total)`. Is `len(borrowers) + waiters_to_notify <= value` guaranteed? Not necessarily! Example: total=2, borrowed=2, waiters=3. Increase to 5: waiters_to_notify = 3. borrowers 2 + 3 = 5 ≤ 5. OK. So waiters exist only when the limiter is full (or another waiter exists). So `len(borrowers) >= total_old`... wait, `len(borrowers) >= self._total_tokens` at enqueue time. That\'s a lower bound. Hmm, so with the buggy window, borrowed could already exceed. In the normal state, borrowers == old_total exactly (full), so borrowers + waiters_to_notify == value exactly. OK, no overflow. But can borrowers > old_total happen? Only via this very bug. We have to discard it to return the token, and then `_notify_next_waiter()` passes it to the next waiter in line (adding that next waiter to `_borrowers`). The event object is unique per waiter and only set at pop time. Once popped from the queue, no one else will set it. But between the `pop` and the check… there\'s no await, so it\'s atomic. ✓\n\nOne more path to check: `release_on_behalf_of` → `_notify_next_waiter` adds the borrower to `_borrowers` and sets the event. If the woken task later… normal flow: `event.wait()` returns → add (no-op) → done. ✓\n\nWhat about double-release or releasing a non-borrower — no changes.\n\nWhat about `acquire_nowait` on a borrower who\'s already in `_borrowers`? — RuntimeError, no change.\n\nEdge: `total_tokens = math.inf`. `waiters_to_notify = inf - old = inf`; loop `while self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify` — `inf -= 1` stays inf, no problem; loop ends when the queue is empty. Each woken waiter gets reserved. `len(borrowers) < inf` is always true. ✓\n\nNow — a very important consideration — the **eager task factory** (Python 3.12, `asyncio+eager` parameter). Under an eager task factory, coroutines can start executing eagerly… in our scenario, does the eager factory change the timing of the woken waiter? `event.set()` → `fut.set_result(True)` → under an eager task factory, the task\'s step is scheduled; eager tasks start eagerly at `create_task` time, but resumption is still via the event loop. The woken waiter can\'t run until the current task yields. So the window is the same. The test should pass under eager too.\n\nAnother consideration: the docstring of `_notify_next_waiter` says "Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now." With the fix, it *also* reserves the token. Let me update the docstring to reflect that: e.g., "Notify the next task in line and hand it a token if this limiter has free capacity now." Hmm — let me match the project\'s tone. Let me look at how Semaphore\'s release docstring… Semaphore has no docstring. I\'ll update it modestly.\n\nNow, should the setter also call the shared logic? Let me write the final code:\n\n```python\n    @total_tokens.setter\n    def total_tokens(self, value: float) -> None:\n        if not isinstance(value, int) and not math.isinf(value):\n            raise TypeError("total_tokens must be an int or math.inf")\n\n        if value < 0:\n            raise ValueError("total_tokens must be >= 0")\n\n        waiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)\n        self._total_tokens = value\n\n        # Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\n        while self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n            borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n            self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n            event.set()\n            waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\nand\n\n```python\n    def _notify_next_waiter(self) -> None:\n        """Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now."""\n        if self._wait_queue and len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens:\n            borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n            self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n            event.set()\n```\n\nand\n\n```python\n        try:\n            await event.wait()\n        except BaseException:\n            self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n            if event.is_set():\n                self._borrowers.discard(borrower)\n                self._notify_next_waiter()\n\n            raise\n\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n```\n\nHmm, one question: in the exception path, should I use `discard` or `remove`? `discard` is safer (no KeyError if somehow not present). If `event.is_set()`, the borrower was added at pop time — always present. `discard` is fine and idiomatic.\n\nWait, actually — I should re-examine one more scenario in the exception path. The `except BaseException` also catches things other than CancelledError (e.g., GeneratorExit if the task is being cleaned up). If `event.is_set()`, the token was reserved → discard + notify. ✓\n\nBut now, here\'s the subtle one: in the exception path, if `event.is_set()` and the *next* waiter in the queue exists and capacity permits, `_notify_next_waiter()` reserves the token for it. But what if `len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens` after the discard? Then `_notify_next_waiter` does nothing, and the token stays available for nowait acquires. ✓ Correct.\n\nNow — does `acquire_on_behalf_of` need the `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` after `await event.wait()`? If the event is set, the borrower is already in `_borrowers`. The add is a no-op. Should I remove it? If I remove it, the code relies on the invariant that all set() sites add the borrower. That holds. But keeping it makes `acquire_on_behalf_of` self-contained: "when I come out of the wait, I hold the token." Keep it — minimal diff, no behavioral change, defensive. Hmm, but actually, keeping dead-ish code… no, it\'s not dead: it\'s a no-op in the normal path. Minimal-change principle: leave it. No wait — let me reconsider. If I leave it, a future reader might be confused about "when does the borrower get added." A comment could help, but let me not over-annotate. Each woken waiter is immediately added to `_borrowers` at the moment of notification — same reservation semantics as `_notify_next_waiter`.\n2. Therefore, during the window between `event.set()` and the waiter\'s resumption, `available_tokens` already accounts for the reserved tokens, and a concurrent `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` sees the correct free capacity.\n\nLet\'s reason about correctness:\n\n- Before the setter: waiters exist only when the limiter is full (invariant), so `len(borrowers) == old_total` (borrowers can\'t exceed total if the invariant holds… well, it could be smaller, but if waiters exist then by induction `len(borrowers) >= old_total`. Because a waiter can only be enqueued when `self._wait_queue or len(borrowers) >= total`, and by induction, the existence of a queue means full).\n- `waiters_to_notify = value - old_total`.\n- Number of waiters actually notified: `min(len(wait_queue), waiters_to_notify)`.\n- New borrowers count: `len(borrowers) + min(len(wq), value - old_total)`.\n  - Case `len(wq) <= value - old_total`: new = `len(borrowers) + len(wq)`. Since waiters exist, `len(borrowers) >= old_total` → new ≥ old_total + ... wait, this is a lower bound. Upper bound: `len(borrowers) <= old_total` (invariant) → new ≤ old_total + len(wq) ≤ old_total + value - old_total = value. ✓\n  - Case `len(wq) > value - old_total`: new = `len(borrowers) + value - old_total` ≤ old_total + value - old_total = value. ✓\n- So borrowers can never exceed the new total. `available_tokens` never goes negative. And the nowait guard `self._wait_queue or len(borrowers) >= total` correctly raises `WouldBlock` when the queue is still non-empty, and correctly admits exactly `value - old_total - (still reserved)` new borrowers.\n\nAlso, what about the case where total is raised while a previously-woken (by release) waiter is still pending in `_borrowers`? The arithmetic still holds: `len(borrowers) ≤ old_total`… hmm, is that right? If a woken-by-release waiter is pending in `_borrowers` and hasn\'t yet resumed, at the time of release `borrowers == old_total` (one was removed, one was added). So `len(borrowers) ≤ old_total` still holds. ✓\n\nEdge case: what if `total_tokens` is raised *multiple times* or lowered and then raised? Each setter invocation satisfies the same invariant.\n\nEdge case: `total_tokens = math.inf`: `waiters_to_notify = inf`, the loop pops all waiters, each is reserved. borrowers = old_borrowers + all_waiters ≤ old_total + waiters < inf. ✓\n\nNow — one more subtle path worth double-checking: the setter\'s loop does not check `len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens`. No, a fresh process.\n\nSo the answer is: yes, this fix holds on this path as well, because the same "reserve at notification time" invariant is applied to the setter\'s wake-up loop, and the nowait guard reads `_borrowers`, which now includes the reserved woken waiters.\n\nNext, add a test for that path. What should the test verify?\n\nScenario: limiter with 1 token. Main borrows "A". Start waiter "B" → blocks. Raise `total_tokens` to 2. Actually, the partial-wake case is the more interesting arithmetic case: total=1, A holds, B, C wait. total→3: B and C both notified (waiters_to_notify=2, 2 waiters). borrowers={A,B,C}, available 0, nowait X WouldBlock. Or total→2: only B notified, C remains in the queue. borrowers={A,B}, available=0, nowait X → WouldBlock (queue non-empty AND full). C\'s token is not over-counted — before the fix, in the window after the setter: borrowers={A}, available = 2-1 = 1 → X gets admitted → later borrowers {A,B,C,X} = 4 > 2. The test catches that.\n\nI think the partial-wake variant (total 1→2 with 2 waiters) is the strongest single test: it verifies (a) B is reserved at notification time, (b) the nowait guard raises in the window, (c) C is *not* over-reserved (still in the queue, tasks_waiting==1, and the final borrow count is correct).\n\nLet me write it:\n\n```python\n    async def test_increase_tokens_nowait_race(self) -> None:\n        """\n        Test against a race condition: when increasing total_tokens wakes waiting\n        tasks, the granted tokens must be reserved for the woken tasks, so a\n        non-blocking acquire issued before they resume must raise WouldBlock, and\n        the un-woken tasks must not be counted as borrowers.\n        """\n        limiter = CapacityLimiter(1)\n        limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("A")\n        async with create_task_group() as tg:\n            tg.start_soon(limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of, "B")\n            tg.start_soon(limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of, "C")\n            await wait_all_tasks_blocked()\n            limiter.total_tokens = 2\n            # B has been woken and its token reserved, C is still waiting\n            assert limiter.borrowed_tokens == 2\n            assert limiter.available_tokens == 0\n            assert limiter.statistics().tasks_waiting == 1\n            with pytest.raises(WouldBlock):\n                limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")\n            limiter.release_on_behalf_of("A")\n            # The freed token goes to C; no token left for a non-blocking acquire\n            assert limiter.available_tokens == 0\n            with pytest.raises(WouldBlock):\n                limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")\n\n        assert limiter.borrowed_tokens == 0\n        assert limiter.available_tokens == 2\n        assert limiter.statistics().tasks_waiting == 0\n```\n\nWait — carefully, on the second release: after `release_on_behalf_of("A")`: borrowers were {A, B} → now {B}; `_notify_next_waiter`: queue has C, `len(borrowers)=1 < total=2` → C is reserved and popped. borrowers={B, C}, available=0, tasks_waiting=0. Then nowait X → full → WouldBlock. ✓\n\nAfter task group exits: B and C resume, each holds a token, then releases (their `acquire_on_behalf_of` completes, the task ends, and… wait — B and C are running `limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of("B")` as a task body, not `async with`! Once `acquire_on_behalf_of` returns, the task ends and the token is not released! The token is not returned by `acquire_on_behalf_of` — the caller must release.\n\nSo if we start `tg.start_soon(limiter.acquire_on_behalf_of, "B")` as a task body, B acquires and never releases.
